# Decision log

`[ADR]` · Index of every decision, proposal and stated fact from the advisory session (8–9 Nov 2026) and its follow-ups. Newest first within each section.

**Status values:**
- **Decided**: Colin stated or approved it. Binding.
- **Proposed**: advisor proposal, not yet confirmed. Don't act on it without Colin's OK.
- **Open**: a question with no answer yet.
- **Superseded**: replaced by a later entry. Kept for history.
- **Decided (Claude)**: a decision Claude made itself, under D-70 (non-critical matters) or, until v1.0, under D-80 (any matter, critical included). Binding until Colin revises it; Colin can change it at any time.

**Rule:** an entry moves from Proposed to Decided only when Colin says so. Record the date and the wording. Every decision is recorded here, including Claude's own (D-71).

---

| F-26 | 2026-11-13 | Asked in the project chat at 15:17 UTC: "Do you think it is safe to reuse review threads for multiple issues?" The coordinator answered "Partly": safe for related, non-critical PRs in the same lane (the three #74 parts, small follow-ups), riskier for critical PRs, since a long thread loses sharpness and the reviewer starts trusting its own earlier reading; it recommended one review thread per issue, reused across that issue's PRs, and always a fresh thread for critical PRs from a different issue. Colin: "yes" | D-376 |
| F-25 | 2026-11-13 | Approved in the project chat at 14:53 UTC the coordinator's three workflow proposals of 14:52 UTC (one KB PR per day for all decision-log entries; quarantining a test that fails twice without a code cause, every quarantined test fixed before v1.0; at most three implementation threads at once, one per lane): "all 3 suggestions approved" | D-373, D-374, D-375 |
| F-24 | 2026-11-12 | Wrote in the project chat at 17:52 UTC, while the plan for layout step 3 (block kinds and sections) was waiting for his approval: "From now on you will review and merge all PRs and work totally by yourself until v1.0 is reached. Just inform me about all decisions on the way. Be strict with testing, reviewing and keep the current processes. Whenever you feel uncertain about something, you can use fable 5.1. on medium or high if needed." | D-80, D-81 |
| F-23 | 2026-11-12 | Asked in the project chat: "Do you think is should also let you merge the decision log changes?" Claude (coordinator) answered yes, with one split: a decision-log entry that only records what Colin already said in chat is a transcript, so a second merge by him adds no safety; Claude merges those after a review thread confirms they match his words. Entries recording Claude's own decisions and any other KB change (architecture, playbooks, rules) still wait for him. Colin: "yes" | D-78 |
| F-22 | 2026-11-12 | Reviewing sci2audio PR #29 (layout step 1): Claude's review raised three questions for Colin. (1) Layout finds each furniture line's role (page number, running head, …) but keeps only the kind `furniture`, so printed page numbers are lost. (2) The PR has about 960 non-generated lines, well over the ~400-line guideline in CLAUDE.md; split it? (3) `.claude/rules/tests.md` has no "first of its kind" row for `layout/`. Colin: "D1: agree / D2: drop the 400 lines PR limit. only split when reasonable / D3: yes" | D-74, D-75, D-76 |
| F-21 | 2026-11-12 | Set the coordination process: "From now on you, the project coordinator use fable 5.1. Whenever a new thread is started use opus 5.5 on high, except it's a critical issue, than use fable 5.1 on medium. For non critical decisions you are allowed to make the decision and also merge PRs. Always review before merging in a new thread using opus on high. For critical issues review but dont make decisions and dont merge without my approval." Claude (coordinator) raised three points: (1) "critical" was undefined, and the `risk:high` label alone can't be the line because the PR author sets it; proposed a definition by what a change touches; (2) Fable on medium for critical issues points the wrong way, since critical work needs the most effort; proposed Opus 5.5 on high everywhere, Fable only for the coordinator; (3) a merge Colin never sees is a risk; proposed one project-chat line per merge. Also read the plan-first rule (D-62) as still binding for merges. Colin answered: "1. yes 2. agree 3. agree. also keep your own decisions in the log", then: "plans do not wait for me, if you have a high confidence that your sugggested change is superior to others." and "I always want to be informed about architectural decisions" | D-68…D-73 |
| F-20 | 2026-11-12 | Reviewing sci2audio PR #26 (PDF extraction): noted, among other points, that a gap limit of 1 em can split a justified line into two. Claude measured it on the corpus (1–9% of lines split, e.g. "2.1" apart from "Probing Models for Knowledge"; 1.5 em would instead merge table cells) and asked where split lines are joined. Also asked Claude to always say exactly what is fixed and why, and to create an issue for anything deferred | D-67 |
| F-19 | 2026-11-12 | Reviewing the layout plan (Issue #21): asked whether something should be done before it. Agreed with Claude's advice to build recording-only PDF extraction first, so layout is tuned and tested on real papers. Asked whether there are other options than pypdfium2 and pdfminer.six and about combining approaches; then chose "Approve with pypdfium2" and asked for issues for the two combining ideas (bookmark outline as a heading hint, a layout model as second opinion) | D-65, D-66 |
| F-18 | 2026-11-12 | Plan for Issue #16 (corpus ground truth): the issue named YAML files, but reading YAML needs PyYAML, a new dev dependency that also touches `pyproject.toml` (infra lane); Claude proposed TOML, read with Python's built-in `tomllib`. Colin chose "Approve, use TOML" on the decision card | D-64 |
| F-17 | 2026-11-12 | Reviewing the core plan (Issue #17): asked how images, tables and graphics are handled and whether layout is saved, e.g. for a scientific poster without clear columns. Then: "The reading order should not be decided in the extraction part. I want the extraction to extract all parts of a document and save them in a structured way. In my opinion we also then need positioning data, font sizes, bold italic and whatever maintained in the document. Another layer in our pipeline should decide the order of reading." | D-63; revises the plan for Issue #17 (D-61) |

Decided: the installers workflow builds a minimal audio-only FFmpeg (LAME MP3, native AAC, ipod/M4B muxer, WAV input, cover images) from pinned source tarballs per system, LGPL with LAME; IMAGEIO_FFMPEG_EXE points at it and imageio's binary is left out. The source archive holds FFmpeg, LAME, espeak-ng and the build script. Fast output tests and the speaking smoke test run against this build. PyPI installs unchanged (D-120). | D-733 | 2026-11-17 | Resume plan approved: Resume keeps the run's overwrite choice | `plans/resume-build-plan.md` is approved as written, including item 4 (the cache button behind a confirm box). Diagnosis: progress is not lost with "Diese Dateien ersetzen" (the spoken chunks are reused); only the cache button loses it. Cause: Resume posts overwrite=false although the paused run started with overwrite=true, and JobRecord keeps no record of it, so a paused replace run hits the existing-files check again. Fix: JobRecord and JobOut gain overwrite (additive); the job card carries data-overwrite and Resume reposts it. The existing-files card drops the cache button and says the spoken sentences are kept (only when the cache holds some). The cache button sits behind a confirm box that names the loss. Additions: the confirm-box test must fail without the confirm step; the empty-cache case is tested. Labels lane:app, lane:web, risk:high, bug; milestone v1.3. Colin was given the workaround at 08:30 UTC. Merge note: PR #505 (Resume after Pause continues a replace run; freeing the cache asks first; #504) had one round with no findings (`plans/review-pr505-round1.md`): 13 of 13 mutants caught, the nightly seeds pass, three systems green. | D-678 | 2026-11-15 | v1.1 release checklist runs: fixes and two accepted points | The release/1.1 checklist runs (ZAP, nightly, mutation; D-669) are in `plans/release-v11-checklist-runs.md` (project files). ZAP passed. The nightly and mutation failures are fixed on the branch release-fix/1.1.0-nightly (5131ba1): the EPUB cover test passes `--chapters all` because of #190; the Firefox position test lets the first server's requests through; the Firefox speed test reads its options through `page.evaluate`; mutmut's `also_copy` includes `.github/`. Batch B (PR 2): #389 the library and the reading shelf, #390 Voices, Privacy, not found and removal. Batch C (PR 3): #391 Prepare with the job inline (folds in #365). Batch D (PR 4): #392 the Listen sheet and sweep, #393 resume bar, context panel and dock (folds in #314), #394 Focus and the shortcuts sheet. Later: #400 keep playing between pages (D-664's revisit), #401 write the finished chapters when a job fails. Batches A to D run in order in one web-lane thread (Opus high), one PR per batch, with a fitness check before each batch; one review thread for the series, Opus high for A and medium for B to D. The study layer starts after batch D and its ADR; its scope is Colin's. Choices adopted: while making, Pause is today's cancel (everything kept), Resume reruns from the speech cache, and Cancel also frees the cache after a confirm, with no change to the job runner. A failure says what is really kept; a partial audiobook is #401. The Listen "Changes" panel is read-only. Strikes and "said as" come from the job's recorded settings, not today's switches. Study-layer exports are files first; the ADR may propose a user-started local connection to Zotero or Anki as a later option, because such a call is a network call under the hard rule. | D-626 | 2026-11-15 | A workflow that spends fewer tokens | Colin's answers to the coordinator's six proposals, which the coordinator wrote into the project instructions. (1) PRs are batched by lane: extraction/voices, layout, textprep, app/release, with at most three active at once. A lane thread lives for one batch (its plan and its PRs), and the coordinator replaces it when the PRs are merged or its conversation gets long. Implementation stays on Opus high. (2) One review thread per issue batch handles all its PRs and rounds. Effort is set when a thread starts: medium for non-critical reviews, high for critical ones. Heavy checks (mutation, nightly seeds, three-system CI) run only for critical PRs on the final round, and move into label-triggered GitHub workflows where possible. (3) At most 2 review rounds, and leftovers become issues. Critical PRs may go up to round 4, then wait for Colin's approval. (4) KB PRs are merged without review, one per day, by one long-lived KB thread. (5) Every new thread first reads the newest changes in the KB, the plans and the decision log. The coordinator decides when to start a new thread before a conversation gets too long. (6) The coordinator reports only when Colin asks about progress or a decision is critical. Colin's words: "1: approved. … 3: approved, except if the issue is critical - then continue until 4 - after that stop and ask for my approval 4: merge KB PRs without review. all KB PRs should use the same thread … 5: approved. make sure that before the work starts, it reads the newest changes in the KB, plans and decision log. Start a new thread before the conversation gets too long. you decide when it's the right point to start a new thread. 6. report only when i ask about the progress or if some decision was critical." Work resumed at 13:36:29 UTC ("continue the before started threads, don't start new ones yet"), so the D-611 freeze on new threads stays. | **Decided** (Colin, 15 Nov 10:43:15 UTC; the coordinator's answers to his questions were confirmed in the project chat at 2026-11-15 10:44:10 UTC); **amends D-68, D-73, D-373, D-375, D-376** and the chat line per decision of D-80 and D-462; **amended by D-705** (16 Nov): one implementation thread and its review thread at a time; refined by D-702, D-703, D-712 and D-714 | project instructions (15 Nov); D-68, D-73, D-80, D-373, D-375, D-376, D-462, D-611 |

Not approvable yet, with no blocker. S1: the plan's "German fraction words likewise" is missing. In German, a fraction before a unit chain that ends in a letter power reads differently on the two runs ("2/3 m s⁻²" reads "2 Drittel m s⁻²", then "2 Drittel m s hoch minus 2"). Both runs must agree. Option (a) is recommended: `numbers_de._fractions` leaves such a fraction as printed, as it already does when a letter power follows directly ("1/5 m⁶"). A German regression case covers "2/3 m s⁻²", "3/4 m s⁻¹" and "3/4 kg m²", with "1/4 cm²" as a control. | D-606 | 2026-11-15 | SRT and WebVTT subtitles plan | The plan `plans/issue214-subtitles-plan.md` (project files) for #214 is approved, as one critical PR (two golden files change: the CLI help and `"subtitles": []` in the audio record). The spoken text travels from `app.audio.assemble` into `output.model.Spoken.text`. Text the voice failed on (D-162) has no cue. A new pure module `output/subtitles.py` builds cues inside `write_audiobook` from the same Timeline as the read-along. A cue never spans two chunks, so cues end at sentence ends. A cue has at most 2 lines of 42 characters. Longer chunks are cut at clause marks, with the time shared by characters. A chunk's outer times equal the read-along's. One `.srt` and one `.vtt` per chapter go beside the MP3s, and one pair for the book beside the M4B. They are written atomically before the read-along file, and the existing-files check covers them (D-163). Subtitles are off by default and remembered (`JobSettings.subtitles`, passed into `OutputSettings`). The CLI gets `--subtitles` and the web form a checkbox. The validators are the bundled ffmpeg and Chromium's WebVTT parser, with no new dependency. Required additions: a Hypothesis test asserts contiguous, non-overlapping cues per chunk with exact outer times, and follow-up issues are filed for a web-player track (#351, v1.2) and word-level times (#352, Later). PR #357 opened at head 9420cd8. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 15 Nov 08:08 UTC, under D-80 as extended by D-462 and D-553; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-15 08:08:14 UTC); follows D-453, D-162, D-163 | sci2audio Issue [#214](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/214), Issue [#351](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/351), Issue [#352](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/352), PR [#357](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/357) (merged as 87c6503); `plans/issue214-subtitles-plan.md` (project files); D-162, D-163, D-453 |

| D-602 | 2026-11-14 | The ZAP scan flake is not quarantined | The ZAP scan flake in CI is not quarantined, because it is a security scan, not a test. The flake is #177 (v1.2: ci: ZAP scan walk sometimes fails at "Replace these files" (files exist already)). It has been seen three times, the last on PR #345 head d599b50, which does not touch web code. The thread on #345 documented the likely cause on #177. Closes #28. 9. freeze_support in cli.main. | D-580 | 2026-11-14 | Keep CI usage lean | Keep CI usage lean. Background: GitHub Actions showed $51.92 gross on 14 Nov, two thirds of it macOS, billed $0 because the repo is public. Colin's words, in the project chat at 17:16:55 UTC: "Lets use this advice:" followed by pasted advice with three points: 1. "Gate macOS and Windows builds" to tags, main merges or workflow_dispatch, with the fast tests and lint on ubuntu-latest for every push and PR. 2. A `concurrency` block with cancel-in-progress. 3. "Cache dependencies aggressively". The coordinator turned this into rules (its implementation details, 17:17Z): Lint and the fast suite run on ubuntu-latest for every push and PR. macOS and Windows run only on pushes to main and release/*, on v* tags, on workflow_dispatch, and on PRs labelled "ci:full". Review threads add that label before merging, so the three-platform merge rule stays. Concurrency cancels in-progress runs per workflow and ref, except for the release workflow and main. Dependency caching is checked on all three runners. The leftover-bugs thread (app/release lane) implements it as a critical PR. If branch protection names the macOS or Windows checks as required, Colin must change that by hand. Addendum (coordinator 17:19Z, no new number): the plan `plans/ci-lean-plan.md` (project files) is approved. Its details: The only required check is "CI passed", so branch protection does not change. There is no CI on tags, because release.yml already smoke-tests the wheel on all three systems. This replaces "v* tags" in the rules above. cancel-in-progress applies only to PR runs and zap. Nightly and mutation use non-cancelling groups; release has no group. cache-python is on everywhere except release.yml. Playwright Chromium is cached by version. The "ci:full" label starts the three-system run through a labeled trigger. CLAUDE.md step 8 is updated for review threads. Pushes run CI only on main and release/**; other branches are tested through their PR. | **Decided** (Colin, 14 Nov 17:16:55 UTC, the pasted advice); the rule details are the coordinator's reading, confirmed to Colin in the project chat at 2026-11-14 17:17:17 UTC; **amends D-224**; **refined by D-620** (15 Nov): ci:full starts the three-system run through a dispatch workflow, not a labeled trigger; carried out by D-643 (15 Nov): PR #364 merged as 3d66b93 | sci2audio PR [#325](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/325) (merged as e3610f4); `plans/ci-lean-plan.md` (project files); D-224 |
| D-579 | 2026-11-14 | Input size limits for text, Markdown, EPUB and covers | The input-limits plan is approved (`plans/input-limits-plan.md` (project files)). It covers #174, #158 and #165, and #298 rides along. The work is one PR, not critical, extraction only. The limits: MAX_LINES: 500,000 for text and Markdown. MAX_MARKS: 1,000,000 Markdown inline marks. MAX_ITEMS: 1,000,000 lines, runs and pictures per EPUB or Markdown book (the PageReader budget). Covers: at most 25 MP and 10,000 px a side, read from the PNG IHDR or JPEG SOF with the standard library only. How the limits work: The counts happen before the expensive parse. There is no "<" count, because of MathML and SVG chapters. Tag tuples are interned in PageReader. ExtractionError names the limit (D-347/D-315 style, details in brackets per D-394). An oversized cover means no cover (as D-313). The worst case per limit is about 0.2–1 GB and about 20 s, instead of 14–45 GB. Addendum (coordinator 17:21Z, no new number). The measured worst cases were higher than planned: 500,000 Markdown list lines took 1,262 MB, and 1,868 MB with emphasis; table cells and strikethrough were not counted. So Markdown gets its own limits: MAX_LINES 250,000 and MAX_MARKS 500,000, with "\|" and "~" counted as marks. Text stays at 500,000 lines. The Markdown worst case is back to about 950 MB. For headroom, a 10 M character hard-wrapped book is about 160,000 lines and 130,000 marks. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 14 Nov 17:07 UTC, under D-80 as extended by D-462 and D-553; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-14 17:06:53 UTC); follows D-572 (2) | sci2audio Issue [#174](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/174), Issue [#158](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/158), Issue [#165](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/165), Issue [#298](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/298), PR [#327](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/327) (merged as fbc6de8); `plans/input-limits-plan.md` (project files); D-313, D-315, D-347, D-394, D-572 |

A hanging separator goes along with its removal. No removal may glue a full stop to a number. **B, numbers** (#304 #310 #318): a fraction after a minus is read on the first run; "~" reads "about" at a hyphen line break; a German date without a year is read before "1/2"; a range with a yearless end is read with "bis". | D-575 | 2026-11-14 | Inline fractions read numerator and denominator apart (#202) | The #202 plan is approved (`plans/issue202-inline-fraction-plan.md` (project files)): Q1a: an inline fraction with a drawn bar reads its numerator and denominator with a space between them. Q2a: the same rule lives in two places, following the D-274/#128 M2 precedent. Extraction keeps PDFium's generated space, and layout puts a space between the pieces. A fraction bar is a Drawing at most 0.15 em thick and at least 3× wider than it is thick, with text above and below it within 0.6 em. Layout uses LayoutSettings.fraction_bar and fraction_reach; extraction uses the constants FRACTION_BAR and FRACTION_REACH. Q4: extraction never adds a space, per D-276. Q5: the digit-under-digit rule is unchanged. The work is one PR, critical (the liu golden files change) and risk:high. Reading the bar as "over" stays with spoken formulas (D-460). Addendum (coordinator 17:07Z, no new number; PR #323 review round 1, notes `plans/review-pr323-round1.md` (project files)): fraction_bar is raised to 0.25 em in both extraction and layout. TeX keeps the bar's thickness when it sets a fraction smaller: liu's 0.8 pt bar over 5 pt letters is 0.16 em. There are no false positives, because underlines and table rules are thinner. The reach stays 0.6 em but is now measured from the letter nearest the bar. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 14 Nov 16:42 UTC, under D-80 as extended by D-462 and D-553; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-14 16:41:51 UTC); follows D-572 | sci2audio Issue [#202](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/202), Issue [#128](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/128), PR [#323](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/323) (merged as 334e5a8); `plans/issue202-inline-fraction-plan.md` (project files); `plans/review-pr323-round1.md` (project files); D-274, D-276, D-460, D-572 |

All six recommendations are approved: (1) The feature ships as its own release right after v1.2, before installers and the public plugin interface. (2) By default, terms the paper defines itself are said in full on their first use in each chapter. (3) No LLM in the first two phases. (4) German is expanded only where no word ending changes. (5) Rewind-to-expand comes in the last phase and works only in sci2audio's own player. (6) Word-level highlighting comes later, together with page-image read-along (#155). Added by Colin: the user's word list is kept entirely by the user, with no suggestions. (The coordinator reads this as "no built-in lists" too.) An abbreviation the paper does not explain is assumed well known in context and is not expanded. Colin's words, in the design thread at 16:37:28 UTC: "all 6 recommendations approved. A word list is a good idea, but it should be totally maintained be the user. no suggestions. | D-558 | 2026-11-14 | A footnote is moved to after the sentence holding its mark | A new `EditKind.MOVE` with an optional `Edit.to` target position (Q1). Validity rules in `check_edits`: the span is a whole, non-empty block, the target lies in another block, and there is at most one move per block (Q2). Moves run last, in a stage of their own (`Stage.MOVE`), set aside on conflict, and TEXT_STEP_API stays 1 (Q3). A note is read after the sentence that holds its mark, else at the block's end (Q4). Every note whose mark the footnotes step removes from running text moves; the first linked mark wins, with no chains (Q5). Chapters follow where a note is read (`StepRun.read_at`) (Q6). There is no new switch; the move belongs to "Footnotes" (Q7). The player shows a moved note right after its mark's block (Q8). The report gets rows of kind move (Q9). There is no new pause kind (Q10). The work comes as two stacked critical PRs: A (core, runner, app, web; no golden change) and B (the step, regression cases, golden files), reviewed in one thread. Context: #213 "core, textprep: read a footnote next to its mark" (v1.2; TP-05, D-457, D-61 (1)): the first v1.2 work under D-553. | D-554 | 2026-11-14 | Listeners choose the pace of spelled abbreviations | The voice slows only the sounds of spelled letters, not via a textprep rewrite (Q1). There are three paces, which multiply with the voice speed: Normal 1.0, Slower 0.7 and Slowest 0.5 (Q2). The default is Normal, which keeps the audio byte-identical to today and cached audio valid (Q3). What counts as spelled (Q4): in English, a misaki token of 2 or more capitals (plus an optional plural "s") whose phonemes are misaki's own letter spelling; in German, a word of 2 or more capitals whose espeak-ng phonemes equal its letters said one by one. Mixed case, single letters and dotted forms are not slowed. An optional voice-contract parameter `letter_speed` (0.25 to 1.0) is added, and API_VERSION stays 1.0 (Q5); Kokoro implements it (Q6). The app setting is `letter_pace` in JobSettings (Q7), the CLI flag is `--letter-pace {normal,slower,slowest}` (Q8), and the web gets a radio group "Spelled-out abbreviations" with a preview (Q9). One critical PR; three golden snapshot files change. Context: #295 "Let listeners set how fast spelled abbreviations are spoken" (v1.0.1; feature, risk:high, lane:voices), from Colin's third listening-test finding (14:00:08 UTC). | D-547 | 2026-11-14 | Bracketed grant numbers are not read (#285) | #285 "textprep: a grant or award number in brackets is not read" (v1.0.1; bug, risk:high, textprep), from Colin's second listening-test finding (13:15:38 UTC). The plan is approved (`plans/issue285-bracketed-identifiers-plan.md` (project files)). It is critical. A round bracket that holds only identifiers is left out by the voice: a bare run of 5 or more digits, or a letter-digit code with a run of 5 or more digits, optionally after a funding word. This happens inside the citations step, under TP-03 (the Citations switch). The script, read-along and review keep the printed text, and the report gives the reason. Still read: years "(2019)"/"(2019a)", small numbers, grouped numbers "(12,345)", labelled quantities "(n = 12345)", "(5 %)", and short-number names "(OPP-115)", "(GPT-3)", "(COVID-19)". An accepted edge case: an ungrouped bare count such as "participants (12345)" is dropped too. Also: #286 "player: the read-along shows a word joined at a line end without its hyphen" (v1.0.1, lane:web). The read-along shows "con-tributing" because the view keeps a line-end hyphen that the hyphens step joined, while the voice already says "contributing". The fix is to show the joined word, as its own small PR from the code-blocks thread. Record: Colin asked for the rule himself ("if they are not obious years, than exclude them", 13:15:38), so the core can be cited as his words, with the plan's details (thresholds, the kept cases) as Decided (Claude). | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 14 Nov 13:18 UTC, under D-80 as extended by D-462; plan approved; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-14 13:18:40 UTC) | sci2audio Issue [#285](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/285), Issue [#286](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/286), PR [#290](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/290) (merged as 1d4fb56), PR [#300](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/300) (merged as 1f6b4ba); `plans/issue285-bracketed-identifiers-plan.md` (project files); D-19 |

| D-541 | 2026-11-14 | The #271 nested-citations plan is approved | #271: the plan is approved (`plans/issue271-nested-citations-plan.md` (project files)). It is critical because English rules change too. The citations step decides every bracket once, inner first, iteratively. An outer bracket is judged again on what remains after its inner citations are removed, and it goes in the first run if that residual is all citations. Names in subject position stay: "((Smith, 2019) Jones, 2020) is cited" → "Jones is cited". Conditions: the re-judgement sees only inner-citation removal; the corpus's first-run output stays byte-identical except for real nested citations; and a pin checks that a nest with a prose residual keeps the prose. Weighed and rejected: running the step to a fixpoint. The case "((Smith, 2019); Jones, 2020)" → "()" goes to v1.2 issue #278 "textprep: a bracket of a nested citation and a second citation is read as "()"". | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 14 Nov 12:58 UTC, under D-80 as extended by D-462; plan approved with conditions; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-14 13:12:40 UTC) | sci2audio Issue [#271](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/271), Issue [#278](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/278), PR [#279](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/279) (merged as 34303e1); `plans/issue271-nested-citations-plan.md` (project files) |

Before the citations step removes a round-bracket "numeric aside", every square bracket inside it gets the #101 German interval check: "[1,2; 3,4]", with ";" and a decimal comma, is an interval, not a source list. If any inner bracket is an interval, the round bracket stays and each inner bracket is judged on its own. The English path is unchanged. | D-493 | 2026-11-14 | Three German textprep gaps from PR #228 are deferred | From PR #228 (#113 German abbreviations, symbols and units) review round 1 (notes `plans/review-pr228-round1.md` (project files)). Three German textprep gaps are deferred rather than fixed in #228: #229 (v1.1): the sentence end is lost after a unit printed with a full stop mid-block ("2 Std. Danach"). It needs a measured rule, because "2 Mio. Euro" must stay one sentence. It is in the same area as #227 "German sentences are cut after ordinals and abbreviations left as printed". #230 (v1.2): a unit symbol after "pro"/"per" with no number ("pro m²"), in English and German. #231 (v1.2): German "f." after a page number reads "folgende". Added in review round 2 (coordinator 01:42): #233 (v1.2) "textprep: German "p. a." and "1 %-Hürde" readings". Ten in-PR fixes were asked instead: the case of "ein" before units after über/auf/ab, umlaut capitals, ‰, "1 %"/"1 €", "1-kg-Packung", "ff.", "min.", a dangling data-file path, and smaller gaps. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 14 Nov 01:28 UTC, under D-80 as extended by D-462; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-14 06:35:31 UTC (one batched post for the held items)) | sci2audio Issue [#113](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/113), Issue [#229](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/229), Issue [#227](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/227), Issue [#230](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/230), Issue [#231](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/231), Issue [#233](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/233), PR [#228](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/228) (merged as 9d92c26); `plans/review-pr228-round1.md` (project files) |

| D-490 | 2026-11-14 | A release/1.0 branch is cut; v1.1 PRs go to main | #80 release. PR #222 (v1.1 German hyphens) merged to main before the release PRs, so a 1.0.0 tagged on main would ship it, and every later v1.1 merge too. Decision: #222 ships in 1.0.0, since English output is byte-identical; it gets a changelog line in PR #225. After #225 merges, a branch release/1.0 is cut from its merge commit. v1.1 PRs merge to main only. The tag v1.0.0 goes on release/1.0. The release-day PR (changelog date, listening-test file, M4 speed rows) targets release/1.0 and is merged forward into main by a PR. The alternative, holding all v1.1 merges until Colin's hand steps are done, was rejected because it would stall v1.1 for a day or more. Refines D-484 and D-488 (where the tag goes); add notes there. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 14 Nov 01:21 UTC, under D-80 as extended by D-462; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-14 06:35:31 UTC (one batched post for the held items)) | sci2audio Issue [#80](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/80), PR [#222](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/222) (merged as 9af111e), PR [#225](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/225) (merged as b25f192); D-484, D-488 |

| D-467 | 2026-11-13 | The German voice is Kokoro fine-tuned by Thorsten-Voice | (Q1) the German voice is Kokoro-82M fine-tuned by Thorsten-Voice (`Thorsten-Voice/Kokoro`, epoch 5, pinned revision 734e593d, voice id `dm_thorsten`). Its weights are Apache-2.0 and its dataset (Thorsten-Voice) is CC0. It uses the same stack as the English voice, with no new Python dependency. Measured WER is 1.8% with Whisper large-v3-turbo (1.2% with small), against 3.0% (7.3%) for Piper. Speed is 86 characters/s on 4 vCPUs, and the download is 328 MB. The runner-up is Piper `de_DE-thorsten-high`. Kyutai Pocket TTS was refused because its German training data and voice source are undocumented (licence rule). Context: #211 "voices: a German voice (search for the best free offline option and pick it)"; plan `plans/issue211-german-voice-plan.md` (project files), section 7, approved as recommended. | D-434 | 2026-11-13 | Blocks stay in page order after a paragraph is cut | Layout lets a paragraph run on across a page break, and what it meets meanwhile (figures, furniture, footnotes, lines without a position) waits behind it; when a later step (`find_formulas`, lists, captions, graphics) cuts that paragraph so the piece on the next page is a block of its own, a waiting block of the earlier page ends up after a block of the later page. **Fix: one pass in `layout/build.py`, after `find_lists` and before `find_headings`: a block whose first item is on an earlier page than the block before it moves back to just after the nearest earlier block on its own page or before. Blocks already in page order don't move.** Question: how is page order kept after a cut (sci2audio Issue #196, found by the nightly property tests; also nowak p9's page number)? Alternatives not chosen: lines without a position end the open paragraph (fixes only the Hypothesis case, not nowak, and changes how such pages read); each cutting step re-seats what followed the block it cut (the same rule four times, and a future cutting step would have to remember it); loosening the property test (it would bless "65" being read after p10's text). Reason: one pass holds the invariant for every cutting step. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 13 Nov 21:01 UTC, under D-80; plan approved as recommended; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-13 21:01:01 UTC) | sci2audio Issue [#196](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/196), PR [#200](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/200) (merged as b09f18f); plan `plans/issue196-page-order-plan.md` (project files) (Q1); D-432 |

| D-431 | 2026-11-13 | A drawing on an algorithm caption's line is claimed once | For sci2audio Issue [#123](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/123) (layout crash: "items in more than one block"): an algorithm caption reads its float as the walk below it plus the line just above it (`_box_top`), and with a zero-height caption box and a zero-height drawing at the same y, `_beyond` counts the drawing as both below and above, so it landed in the FIGURE block twice. **Fix at the two walks:** `_box_top` skips an item the walk below already took (a de-duplication guard in `_read_float` was planned too, and dropped by D-433); **the shared above/below geometry (`_beyond`) is unchanged.** Question: where is the double claim fixed? Alternative not chosen: make "below" and "above" exclusive for touching edges in `_beyond` (real pages have lines that touch exactly, such as a rule on a caption's baseline, and changing what counts as below moves the walk on corpus pages, risking golden changes for a degenerate case). Reason: the narrowest fix; the walks stay as they are on every real page, and the corpus goldens must stay byte-identical (checked by sha256). | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 13 Nov 20:46 UTC, under D-80; plan approved as written; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-13 20:46:13 UTC); **amended by D-433** (13 Nov): no de-duplication guard | sci2audio Issue #123, PR [#197](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/197) (merged as 14513b2); plan `plans/issue123-layout-crash-plan.md` (project files) |

| D-428 | 2026-11-13 | Footnote marks in EPUB and Markdown are found by their link tags | `FootnoteStep` (same step, category "Footnotes", on by default) gains a **linked-mark pass** before the printed-label pass: **a run tagged `role:doc-noteref` or `epub:noteref` whose `link:` target is an item of a FOOTNOTE block is a mark**, removed with one `remove` edit (consecutive runs with the same noteref and link are one mark); no page, size, word-before or maths condition, since the tag says what the run is. A note with label "" that a linked mark points at is introduced as "Footnote <n>." (D-425, D-144's wording). **The printed-label rule (D-143) skips noteref runs**, and a linked mark of a labelled note counts as that label's one mark on its page, so one step never proposes two edits for one mark. Question: how are a note's marks found when its label is "" (D-406)? Measured on PR #192: the Markdown sample loses its marks ("our own.1", "Do footnotes2", "kept up.2") and its notes read "Footnote 1." and "Footnote 2."; in The Time Machine "sloped at all.1 The end" becomes two utterances and the endnote loses its "↩"; the seven PDF scripts stay byte-identical (PDF runs carry no tags). Refines D-143 and D-144 for tagged files. | D-419 | 2026-11-13 | When a PDF counts as cut short (amends D-395) | Amends D-395 as implemented in sci2audio PR #188: a PDF is refused as **cut short** when it starts with `%PDF-` within its first 1,024 bytes and either **(a)** object data matching `(?<!\d)\d++\s++\d++\s++obj\b` (such as "12 0 obj") **follows its last `%%EOF`**, or **(b)** it has **no `%%EOF` anywhere** and PDFium either rebuilt the cross-reference table (`FPDF_DocumentHasValidCrossReferenceTable` = 0) or could not open it. **Order** when PDFium can't open the file: the password message, then DRM or certificate (D-397), then cut short (rule a, or b with the table counted as rebuilt), else "not a PDF, or the file is damaged". When PDFium opens it, cut short is the **first pre-read check**, before owner permissions (D-396), no pages and the page limit (D-394). Bytes after a whole file's last `%%EOF` that hold no object (zeros, a web page) are not a cut. Question: what exactly marks a cut-short PDF, and where does the check stand among the refusals? Trade-off accepted: a whole file without `%%EOF` whose table PDFium rebuilds is refused (among the corpus papers, only linearized nowak with its marker removed). Reason: object data after the last `%%EOF` shows the file went on past its end marker; bytes without objects there do not. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 13 Nov 20:26 UTC, under D-80; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-13 20:26:00 UTC) | sci2audio Issue [#5](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/5), PR [#188](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/188) (merged as 255ce4a); D-394, D-395, D-396, D-397 |

| D-396 | 2026-11-13 | Owner-password files are refused only when both copying and accessibility are forbidden | A PDF with only an owner password is **refused only when it forbids both copying text and accessibility extraction** (`FPDF_GetDocPermissions` bits 5 and 10, security handler revision 3 or later): "{name}: the PDF's owner does not allow its text to be read out; ask for a copy without this restriction". A file that allows either is **read, as now**. Note: pikepdf and qpdf always allow accessibility from security handler revision 3 on, and PDF 2.0 deprecates permission bit 10, so the refusal mainly catches older RC4 or AES-128 files; files restricted under PDF 2.0 are still read, which is the intent. Question: what about owner-password restrictions (today: always read in full)? Alternatives not chosen: always read; refuse whenever copying is forbidden. | D-385 | 2026-11-13 | A short bold line with a rule drawn under it is a heading | For sci2audio Issue #48 (the "ACL 2023 Responsible NLP Checklist" title in chi and hardt is read as a paragraph, since it has the style of a numbered subsection, D-84): after the first numbered heading, an **unnumbered heading candidate** (bold or larger, 1 to 3 lines and at most 20 words, at least 2 letters, not ending in `. : ; ,`) whose own block holds **a rule drawn under its last line** is a **level-1 heading** without a printed number. **No word matching.** The rule must be (1) thin, at most `line_width` em high; (2) under the line, its top between the middle of the last line's box and `heading_rule_gap` em below that box (D-390); (3) across the line, starting no later than the line's text and ending no earlier, within `align` em; (4) longer than the words, reaching at least `heading_rule_reach` em past one end of the text (D-390), so an underlined link does not count. The existing check that a heading stands over text no larger than the body text still applies. Question: how are the checklist titles recognised as headings without matching words? Measured: on both papers a thin rule is drawn across the whole text width under the title, inside the title line's box; on all seven corpus papers the only other bold or larger short paragraph holding a graphic is biggs' "4,5, Derek Lane" (ORCID icons, not thin, before the first numbered heading). Effect: chi and hardt gain a level-1 section "ACL 2023 Responsible NLP Checklist" (hardt's app golden gets a new chapter 13), the other five papers stay identical, and the corpus heading test finds 75 of 75 truth headings. Alternatives not chosen: the line opens a page after the references and appendices with lettered entries below it (fits the checklist's content, not its typography); the first text block on its page (page breaks are accidents of typesetting); a change in column count; an indent of 1 em; the PDF outline (the ACL PDFs carry none); the words "ACL" or "Checklist" (ruled out by the issue and D-84). Known risk: a bold header row of an uncaptioned table over a wide rule, outside a TABLE placeholder; none in the corpus. Reason: the rule is what the page prints to set the title apart. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 13 Nov 17:58 UTC, under D-80; plan approved as recommended; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-13 17:58:38 UTC) | sci2audio Issue [#48](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/48), PR [#180](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/180) (merged as 5aad4ae); plan `plans/issue48-ruled-heading-plan.md` (project files) §2, §3, §4, §7; D-84, D-90, D-329; follow-up Issue #181 (v1.1: a narrow rule under an indented line joins the block before it) |
| D-384 | 2026-11-13 | The time-estimate curve is not extrapolated past the longest measured chunk | Refines how D-380 is applied (the model and its fit stay as decided): **the fitted per-chunk curve is used only up to the longest chunk length measured so far; chunks still to speak that are longer are costed at that cap.** The approach tried in the review costs a chunk longer than the longest measured length L at model(L) · c / L, the per-character cost at the edge of the measured range. Question: what does the estimate do for chunks longer than any it has measured (sci2audio PR #179, review round 1, M1)? Found: replaying a first estimate after 1,000 measured characters drawn only from short chunks (names, affiliations, headings, lists), on 263 measured chunk times of the reviewer's hardt and pymedphys runs, the extrapolated curve gave a 95th-percentile error of 32 to 42 % and a worst case of +88 %, outside the ±25 % budget (D-286); with the cap, 8 to 21 %. On the real runs (pymedphys 15 %, hardt 9 %) nothing changes, since their measured stretch already holds long chunks. Alternative named in the review: the plain rate for chunks longer than L. Reason: a quadratic fitted on short chunks says nothing reliable about long ones. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 13 Nov 17:56 UTC, under D-80; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-13 17:56:02 UTC) | sci2audio Issue [#136](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/136), PR [#179](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/179) (merged as 3a55407) (review round 1, M1; `plans/review-pr179-round1.md`, project files); D-286, D-380, D-382 |

The remaining error comes from the front matter's affiliation chunks, which the voice speaks about 30 % slower than body text of the same length. Question: should the first chapter also be left out of the measurement (sci2audio Issue #136)? Alternative not chosen for v1.0: skip the first chapter. | D-380 | 2026-11-13 | The remaining time comes from a per-chunk cost model | The remaining-time estimate (`seconds_left`, D-159) becomes a **per-chunk cost model**: the seconds a chunk takes ≈ **a + b·characters + q·characters²**, with **non-negative coefficients fitted on the current run**, summed over the chunks still to speak. It replaces D-159's "characters still to speak divided by the measured speed". Question: how is the remaining time estimated so that short papers are not over-estimated early (sci2audio Issue #136)? Measured on the four runs recorded at plan time (pymedphys three times, hardt), the worst error falls from 39 / 32 / 27 / 14 % to 21 / 12 / 7 / 6 % (together with D-381 and D-382); a later fifth run is in D-383. Alternatives not chosen: a constant plus a per-character term only (39 % to 38 %); weighting recent chunks more (no effect); weighting digits; phoneme counts from the voice (changes the voice contract). Reason: a voice call has a fixed cost, and long chunks cost more per character, which a single speed cannot express. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 13 Nov 17:00 UTC, under D-80; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-13 17:00:04 UTC) | sci2audio Issue [#136](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/136), PR [#179](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/179) (merged as 3a55407); D-159, D-381, D-382 |

| D-378 | 2026-11-13 | CI and the release run on a known Python patch level | For sci2audio Issue [#175](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/175) (the standard library's `html.parser.HTMLParser`, under the shared reader `extraction/markup.py` used by EPUB since PR #148 and by Markdown from PR #169, reads **broken markup** differently across CPython patch levels, since security releases changed it from 3.12.11 on and distribution builds backport it): **v1.0 pins CI and the release to a uv-managed current CPython patch level** and **states that minimum in the install docs and the release checklist**, so golden files stay deterministic. **Making the reader handle those cases itself goes to v1.1** under #175. Found in the review of PR #169: macOS CI (CPython 3.12.10) and Linux CI (3.12.16) gave different `Content` for an unclosed HTML comment; the issue lists eight such cases, among them (unclosed comments, `<![CDATA[`, `<!x`, `--!>`, `<textarea>` content, unclosed attribute quotes). Alternative not chosen for v1.0: the reader handles these cases itself (more code and tests in the extraction lane before the release). | D-377 | 2026-11-13 | A per-document limit on lines or blocks waits for v1.1 | sci2audio Issue [#174](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/174), **a limit on lines or blocks per document against huge memory use on pathological Markdown, text or EPUB files**, goes to **v1.1, together with Issue [#158](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/158)** (bound EPUB memory by items, not only bytes). For v1.0 the existing byte caps stay the only bound: 64 MB per text or Markdown file (D-351), 64 MB per EPUB member and 512 MB per EPUB (D-315), and in the web app 200 MB per opened file and 5 MB of pasted text (D-198). Question: does #174 (found in review round 1 of sci2audio PR #169, S1) block v1.0? Found: memory grows linearly with the number of lines, but steeply (Markdown about 2 KB per short paragraph, 400,000 paragraphs in a 1.2 MB file took 28 s and 0.82 GB; plain text about 440 bytes per line), so a 64 MB file of one-character lines would need about 14 GB (text) or 45 GB (Markdown). Alternative not chosen: decide the limit and add it in v1.0. Reason: real books are far from this (1 MB reads in under a second; the corpus EPUB reads in 0.03 s), the limit needs its own decision on a number well above the longest real book, and #158 asks the same question for EPUB, so both are settled together. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 13 Nov 15:31 UTC, under D-80; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-13 15:31:59 UTC); **PDF gets its own page and character limits in D-394** (13 Nov), since these byte caps do not bound it; #174 and #178 moved to v1.2 by D-463; #174 is fixed under D-579 | sci2audio Issues #174, #158; PR #169 (review round 1, S1); D-198, D-315, D-351 |

Question: is it safe to reuse review threads for several issues? Alternative not chosen: a fresh review thread for every PR. Reason: a review thread that reviewed an issue's earlier PRs already knows its plan and decisions, so later PRs of the same issue are checked against them; a different issue's critical change gets a reviewer with no earlier view of the code. | **Decided** (Colin, 13 Nov 15:17 UTC: "yes" to the coordinator's proposal in the project chat; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-13 15:17:31 UTC); **amended by D-626** (15 Nov): one review thread per issue batch, at most 2 rounds (critical up to 4); **retired by D-702 c** (16 Nov): one review thread may serve consecutive batches of the same lane | F-26; D-68, D-69, D-80 |

Hardening that ZAP doesn't flag (for example `Permissions-Policy`, or COEP for the Spectre rule, if the pinned ZAP doesn't raise them) stays out of the #12 PR; whatever a rule raises is fixed there. Question: how are findings handled? Reason: the issue's bar (no high or medium findings) holds, and every accepted Low alert has a recorded reason. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 13 Nov 14:52 UTC, under D-80; plan approved as recommended; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-13 14:52:37 UTC) | sci2audio Issue [#12](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/12); plan `plans/issue12-zap-plan.md` (project files) §3 (Q6); D-367 |

Question: what fails the scan? Alternative not chosen: only High and Medium fail. Reason: a Low alert is cheap to look at now, and the list then documents why each one is fine. | D-366 | 2026-11-13 | Which pages and states the ZAP scan covers | The driven flow visits, **in English and again in German** (after the language switch's GET form, D-321): `/start` with its 303 answer and `Set-Cookie` (the cookie flags); `/` empty and with documents; opening a file by the upload form (a generated PDF) and opening pasted text; the document page while the analysis runs and after it has finished, with the `category`, `chapter` and `text` parameters; the voice page before the voice is installed, the fake download and the page after it; the script and audio job pages with their event streams; the listen page with an audiobook (its MP3 range requests and `audiobook.json`) and with nothing to play; `/privacy`; `/openapi.json`; every GET API endpoint; a request without a cookie (the 401 page in both languages through `Accept-Language`); a request with a wrong `Host` (421). After the flow, the spider runs from `/`. Question: which pages and states are covered? Reason: every page template (`base`, `index`, `document`, `job`, `listen`, `missing`, `privacy`, `voice`) in both languages, and every answer the middleware writes itself. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 13 Nov 14:52 UTC, under D-80; plan approved as recommended; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-13 14:52:37 UTC) | sci2audio Issue [#12](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/12); plan `plans/issue12-zap-plan.md` (project files) §3 (Q3); D-194, D-321 |
| D-365 | 2026-11-13 | ZAP as a proxy with Playwright driving the real flow | A new script **`scripts/zap_scan.py`** (`uv run python scripts/zap_scan.py`) starts the real server in-process on 127.0.0.1 (a temp work folder, the fake voice, a fake voice download and the small download plan; no models, no network calls); starts **ZAP in Docker as a daemon proxy** with `--network host` (the server answers only `Host: 127.0.0.1:<port>`, D-194); **drives the main flow with Playwright Chromium through that proxy**, so ZAP passively scans every real request and answer (the upload POST, the job POSTs, the SSE streams, the MP3 range requests, the language switch); runs ZAP's spider from `/` with the session cookie and waits for the passive scan; and pulls the alerts over ZAP's local JSON API with `httpx` (already a dependency). It writes `zap-report.json`, `zap-report.html` and `zap-summary.md` and exits non-zero on failure. The judging logic is plain Python with fast-suite unit tests on recorded alert JSON (`tests/unit/test_zap_scan.py`); the scan itself never runs inside pytest, since it needs Docker. Question: how is the server driven? Alternatives not chosen: the stock `zap-baseline.py` with the spider only; ZAP's Automation Framework with a fixed list of GET URLs (neither sees the answers to the upload or job POSTs, and the spider's own POSTs are refused with 403, correctly, since it sends the cookie without an `Origin` header). Reason: the only option that scans the upload flow the way a person uses it, for about 150 more lines. A trial run of the stock scan on main 47c86fb found no high, medium or low alert, only three informational ones (D-367). | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 13 Nov 14:52 UTC, under D-80; plan approved as recommended; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-13 14:52:37 UTC) | sci2audio Issue [#12](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/12); plan `plans/issue12-zap-plan.md` (project files) §3 (Q2); D-194 |

Jobs on other documents, a voice preview and the voice download don't block a removal. **While a removal runs, the store treats the id as gone** (`get` answers None, so starting a job answers 404), and **opening the same bytes waits** for the removal and then makes a fresh folder (one `threading.Lock` in the store around the removal and around putting a new file in place). | D-355 | 2026-11-13 | The order of a removal, and one that fails part-way | Under the work-folder lock, removing deletes **first every entry except `source/` and `job.lock`, then `source/`, then `job.lock` and the folder**. On Windows the lock file is closed first, so it is removed just after the lock is released; if that fails, an empty `job.lock` in an otherwise empty folder stays, which holds nothing and the start page doesn't list. A file already gone counts as removed; a read-only file gets its write bit set and is tried once more (Windows refuses to remove read-only files). Anything else that can't be removed (a file another program holds open on Windows, a permission problem) answers **500 `remove-failed` naming the first path left**, and because the source goes last **the document stays listed**, so the person sees it and can press again. Question: in what order, and what if a removal fails part-way? Alternative not chosen: rename the folder to a trash name first and delete that (atomic on POSIX, but Windows can't rename a folder with the open lock file inside, and leftovers would hide in a trash folder the person never sees). Reason: a removal that fails stays visible and can be finished. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 13 Nov 14:50 UTC, under D-80; plan approved as recommended; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-13 14:50:18 UTC) | sci2audio Issue [#120](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/120), PR [#168](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/168); plan `plans/issue120-remove-document-plan.md` (project files) §3 (3); D-161 |

| D-347 | 2026-11-13 | Which text and Markdown files fail, and with which message | Text and Markdown files fail with `ExtractionError("<name>: …")`, never a traceback or a hang: an empty file ("the file is empty"); only white space or only a byte order mark, or Markdown with no text and no picture, such as only front matter or comments ("the file holds no text"); a NUL byte without a UTF-16/32 mark, or U+0000 after decoding ("not a text file"); a file that starts like a PDF ("this is a PDF, not a text file; open it with the .pdf ending"); one that starts like a zip (EPUB, Word, …), PNG, JPEG, GIF or gzip ("not a text file"); larger than 64 MB ("the file is too large", D-351); Markdown nested too deeply for the parser ("the Markdown is nested too deeply to read", D-351); a file that cannot be opened ("cannot be opened (…)", as PDF and EPUB). **Read, not failed:** invalid UTF-8 (Windows-1252, guessed, D-345), bad sequences after a byte order mark (U+FFFD), any Markdown at all (CommonMark never rejects input), unclosed front matter, control characters other than NUL (kept; text steps decide). Question: which files fail, with which message (the Markdown and text part of sci2audio #5)? Reason: a file fails only where no text can be read, and text is never dropped silently. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 13 Nov 14:48 UTC, under D-80; plan approved as recommended; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-13 14:48:34 UTC) | sci2audio Issue [#74](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/74), PRs [#167](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/167) (core, merged as d79eb25), [#169](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/169) (extraction, merged as 5d8fd2f), [#183](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/183) (app, merged as b8e1e3f); plan `plans/issue74-text-input-plan.md` (project files) §8 (q11); sci2audio Issue #5 |
| D-346 | 2026-11-13 | The core records how a text file was decoded | Core `Source` gains two optional fields: **`Source.encoding`** (the WHATWG name the text was read with: "utf-8", "utf-16le", "windows-1252", …; "" for PDF and EPUB) and **`Source.encoding_guessed`** (True only for the Windows-1252 fallback, D-345; `Source` refuses guessed without an encoding). Alternatives not chosen: no core change, the app asks extraction for the encoding by suffix as it does for covers (the file is read and decoded twice, and the exported `Content` doesn't say how it was read); a free-text `Source.notes` (English text inside core, which the interface can't word). Reason: the exported record says how the text was read (keep everything referenceable). | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 13 Nov 14:48 UTC, under D-80; plan approved as recommended; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-13 14:48:34 UTC) | sci2audio Issue [#74](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/74), PRs [#167](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/167) (core, merged as d79eb25), [#169](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/169) (extraction, merged as 5d8fd2f), [#183](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/183) (app, merged as b8e1e3f); plan `plans/issue74-text-input-plan.md` (project files) §8 (q10); D-345 |
| D-345 | 2026-11-13 | How text and Markdown files are decoded | Text and Markdown files are decoded in this order: (1) a **byte order mark** decides (UTF-8, UTF-16 LE/BE, UTF-32 LE/BE), bad sequences becoming U+FFFD as D-315; the file declares it, so it is not a guess; (2) without a mark, **strict UTF-8**; (3) otherwise **Windows-1252 as the WHATWG Encoding Standard decodes it** (what browsers do for "latin1" too): it never fails, its five unassigned bytes become U+0081 and the like, so no byte is lost; marked as guessed (D-346). Question: how is the encoding found? Alternatives not chosen: charset-normalizer (already installed through requests, but its answer can change between versions, which breaks golden files, and it is slow on large files and unreliable on short ones); Latin-1 (loses the curly quotes and dashes Windows writes at 0x91–0x97); failing the file. Known limit: a UTF-8 file with one stray Latin-1 byte is read wholly as Windows-1252; the notice tells the person to check. Reason: v1.0 is English with a German interface, which Windows-1252 covers. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 13 Nov 14:48 UTC, under D-80; plan approved as recommended; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-13 14:48:34 UTC) | sci2audio Issue [#74](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/74), PRs [#167](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/167) (core, merged as d79eb25), [#169](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/169) (extraction, merged as 5d8fd2f), [#183](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/183) (app, merged as b8e1e3f); plan `plans/issue74-text-input-plan.md` (project files) §8 (q9); D-315 |

Line ends `\n`, `\r\n` and `\r`. White space is folded as D-306 (runs of ASCII space, tab and form feed become one space, none at line start or end); no-break spaces and every other character are kept as stored. No bold, italic, font or size; metadata and outline empty. Question: how does plain text become lines? Alternatives not chosen: one text line per paragraph, its lines joined by a space (simplest for #75, but the printed lines of a poem or an address, and line-end hyphens, are lost); white space kept as in `pre` (indentation kept, but every later stage meets leading spaces). Question: how does a Markdown file become lines? Alternative not chosen: walk markdown-it's tokens directly (about 300 more lines, a second tag reader to keep in step with EPUB's, and raw HTML inside Markdown would be read as text). Reason: #75 (layout without positions) then meets one shape, a Markdown file looking to layout exactly like an EPUB chapter; rendering then re-parsing is faithful, since markdown-it escapes `&`, `<`, `>` and `"` in text and the reader unescapes them. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 13 Nov 14:48 UTC, under D-80; plan approved as recommended; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-13 14:48:34 UTC) | sci2audio Issue [#74](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/issues/74), PRs [#167](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/167) (core, merged as d79eb25), [#169](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/169) (extraction, merged as 5d8fd2f), [#183](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/183) (app, merged as b8e1e3f); plan `plans/issue74-text-input-plan.md` (project files) §8 (q4); D-306 to D-310 |
| D-339 | 2026-11-13 | The Markdown dialect | Markdown is read as **CommonMark plus GFM tables, strikethrough (`~~x~~`), footnotes (`[^1]`, Pandoc and GitHub), dollar maths (`$…$`, `$$…$$`, Pandoc, GitHub, Quarto) and YAML front matter**, with **raw HTML on** (the CommonMark default; `<sup>`, `<br>`, `<details>` and comments are common in Markdown). **Off:** typographer (no smart quotes; text as typed), linkify (it needs another package; bare URLs stay text, which the URL step of sci2audio #66 reads), task lists, definition lists and heading anchors (they would invent ids the file does not hold). Question: which parser? | D-321 | 2026-11-13 | How the language switch sends its choice | The switch of D-288 is **a GET form** (`/language?language=de&back=/path`), not a POST. Every answer carries `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` (D-194), so browsers send **`Origin: null` with a form POST**, which the origin check refuses (403); found by the browser tests while implementing #79. The GET is guarded like every page (start cookie, `Sec-Fetch-Site`, `Origin` when sent), sets nothing but the language cookie and redirects only to a local path; a link from another site is cross-site and carries no `SameSite=Strict` start cookie, so it is refused. Question: how does a plain form reach the server when the no-referrer policy blanks its origin? Alternatives not chosen: accepting `Origin: null` with `Sec-Fetch-Site: same-origin` (weakens D-194 for every write); a referrer policy that sends the origin (changes D-194's headers on every answer); switching by script with `fetch` (a control that fails without the script). Reason: no security rule changes for a cookie that only holds a language. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 13 Nov 13:06 UTC, under D-80; change to the approved plan found during implementation; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-13T13:06:20Z) | sci2audio Issue #79, PR [#142](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/142); plan `plans/issue79-languages-plan.md` (project files) §2; D-288, D-194 |

Question: how far back may a stacked script start? | D-315 | 2026-11-13 | Broken EPUBs (#5, EPUB part) | **Fail with a clear `ExtractionError`, never a traceback or hang**: an empty file; not a zip, a truncated zip or an unsupported compression ("not an EPUB, or the file is damaged"); entries encrypted with a password; no `META-INF/container.xml`; a missing package file; a package file that is not XML or declares an entity; an empty spine; a missing spine document; DRM (`rights.xml`, `sinf.xml`, `license.lcpl`, or `encryption.xml` with any algorithm other than the two font-obfuscation ones); a member decompressing past 64 MB, more than 512 MB read in all, or more than 10,000 spine items. **Read, not failed**: font obfuscation only; a content document with invalid UTF-8 or a wrong declared encoding (decoded with U+FFFD for the bad bytes); malformed XHTML; a broken or missing navigation; no metadata; no cover. Question: which damaged EPUBs fail and which are read? Alternative not chosen: fail a file with a wrong encoding (Issue #5 lists it among broken files; but the rest of the book is fine and U+FFFD stays visible in the report). Reason: as PDF extraction records an unmappable glyph; limits bound a hostile file. Alternatives not chosen: an empty page for a missing document (the book plays with a hole in it); `linear="no"` lines tagged `nonlinear` (nothing would read it in v1.0). | D-313 | 2026-11-13 | Cover images | **Not in `Content`.** Extraction offers `extract_cover(path)`, returning a `Cover` or nothing, per format (`Cover(data, media_type)`, defined in `extraction`), registered by the app next to the extractors. **EPUB**: only the cover the file declares (manifest `properties="cover-image"`, else EPUB 2 `<meta name="cover">`), kept only if its bytes are JPEG or PNG and at most 20 MB. **PDF**: page 1 rendered by pypdfium2, longer side 1400 px, written as PNG by a small standard-library writer (no Pillow in the lock). **App**: the audio job writes the cover to the working folder and passes it as `BookInfo.cover`; any failure means no cover, never a failed job. Question: how does the cover reach output (OU-01)? Alternatives not chosen: cover bytes in `Content` (not exportable; images in golden files); a cover path in `Metadata` (the app would open EPUBs itself); Pillow (HPND licence, not on the allowed list as written, a large binary for one image). Reason: keeps `Content` exportable and file reading in extraction. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 13 Nov 08:10 UTC, under D-80; plan approved as recommended; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-13T08:10:59Z) | sci2audio Issue #73; plan `plans/issue73-epub-plan.md` (project files) §5 (q10); D-63 |
| D-312 | 2026-11-13 | EPUB metadata | `title` = first `dc:title` (white space folded); `authors` = every `dc:creator` in order whose role (EPUB 3 `refines` `role`, EPUB 2 `opf:role`) is `aut` or absent, each kept whole; `date` = the date part of the first `dc:date` (`YYYY`, `YYYY-MM` or `YYYY-MM-DD`), as for PDF; `identifier` = the package's `unique-identifier` `dc:identifier`, as stored; `language` = first `dc:language`, as stored. Question: which package metadata fills `Metadata`? Alternatives not chosen: every creator including illustrators and translators (not authors on an audiobook label); an ISBN or DOI preferred among several identifiers (a guess about which one the file means). Reason: what the file declares, as printed. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 13 Nov 08:10 UTC, under D-80; plan approved as recommended; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-13T08:10:59Z) | sci2audio Issue #73; plan `plans/issue73-epub-plan.md` (project files) §5 (q9) |
| D-311 | 2026-11-13 | EPUB navigation fills `Content.outline`; core field `OutlineEntry.item` | The outline comes from the **EPUB 3 navigation document's `nav epub:type="toc"`, else the EPUB 2 NCX**. Level = nesting depth, deeper than 15 levels left out (as D-267). Title = the text of the entry's `a`/`span` or `navLabel/text`, white space folded as for text lines, nothing else changed. Page = the spine position of the linked document; None for a heading without link or a document not in the spine. **`OutlineEntry` gets `item: str = ""`**, the id of the first item at or after the link's anchor, which `Content` checks is on the entry's page. **A broken or missing navigation never fails the file** (as D-268). Question: how does an EPUB's table of contents map onto the outline? Alternatives not chosen: NCX first (EPUB 3 says the nav wins; an NCX may be stale); no anchor field (layout would match titles on the page, which fails when label and heading differ). Reason: exact chapter starts in files that hold several chapters; D-265 foresaw the field. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 13 Nov 08:10 UTC, under D-80; plan approved as recommended; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-13T08:10:59Z) | sci2audio Issue #73, PR [#140](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/140) (PR 1 of 3, core); plan `plans/issue73-epub-plan.md` (project files) §5 (q8); D-263, D-265, D-266 |
| D-310 | 2026-11-13 | Core field `Picture.ref` | `Picture` gets **`ref: str = ""`**: where the file stores the image, e.g. its path inside the EPUB (`epub/images/titlepage.png`); empty for PDF, whose box finds it. Question: how can an EPUB picture, which has no box, be found again? Alternative not chosen: no field (the picture could not be found again, against the `Picture` docstring). Reason: a later export can name or copy figures (keep options open). | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 13 Nov 08:10 UTC, under D-80; plan approved as recommended; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-13T08:10:59Z) | sci2audio Issue #73, PR [#140](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/140) (PR 1 of 3, core); plan `plans/issue73-epub-plan.md` (project files) §4 (q7) |
| D-309 | 2026-11-13 | Core field `Page.order` | `Page` gets **`order: tuple[str, ...] = ()`**: the ids of all the page's items, lines and pictures alike, in the order the file stores them, set only where items have no position (EPUB now, Markdown in #74); empty for PDF, where the boxes place the items. `Page` refuses an order that is not empty and does not list each of its item ids exactly once. Question: how is a picture's place between two lines kept without boxes? Alternatives not chosen: one shared counter in ids (`p3-l1`, `p3-i2`) read by layout (ids become meaningful, and PDF ids differ); pictures recorded as lines (mixes kinds). Reason: #75 needs it to put a figure placeholder before its caption; an explicit field says what it means. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 13 Nov 08:10 UTC, under D-80; plan approved as recommended; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-13T08:10:59Z) | sci2audio Issue #73, PR [#140](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/140) (PR 1 of 3, core); plan `plans/issue73-epub-plan.md` (project files) §4 (q6); D-63 |
| D-308 | 2026-11-13 | Bold and italic in EPUB | **From the HTML default rendering, not the book's CSS**: bold for `b`, `strong`, `th`, `h1`–`h6`; italic for `i`, `em`, `cite`, `dfn`, `var`, `address`. Font stays empty and size 0. Question: how are bold and italic set without fonts? Alternatives not chosen: read the book's CSS (a CSS engine for little gain; tags already carry the meaning); never set them (layout's run-style checks would see nothing on EPUB). Reason: what a reader without the book's style sheet sees. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 13 Nov 08:10 UTC, under D-80; plan approved as recommended; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-13T08:10:59Z) | sci2audio Issue #73; plan `plans/issue73-epub-plan.md` (project files) §4 (q5) |
| D-307 | 2026-11-13 | Tags on EPUB text runs | `TextRun.tags` are the run's ancestors from below `<body>`, outermost first. Each element adds its **name**, then where present: **`#<id>`**, **`epub:<token>`** per `epub:type` token, **`role:<token>`** per `role` token, **`lang:<tag>`** from `lang`/`xml:lang`, **`hidden`**, **`aria-hidden`**, and for an `href` **`link:<item id>`** (resolved after all pages are read: the first item at or after the anchor, or `link:p<n>` for a whole document) or **`href:<as stored>`** (web, mail, or an anchor that does not resolve). `class` and `style` are not recorded. More than 100 nested elements add no further tags. Question: which markup does extraction keep as tags? Alternative not chosen: element names only (#75 needs `epub:type` for notes and asides, ids and links to tie a noteref to its note when chapters number notes from 1). Reason: identifiers kept as printed and options kept open (Colin's principle), while presentational noise stays out. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 13 Nov 08:10 UTC, under D-80; plan approved as recommended; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-13T08:10:59Z) | sci2audio Issue #73; plan `plans/issue73-epub-plan.md` (project files) §4 (q4); D-148 |
| D-306 | 2026-11-13 | What a text line is in an EPUB | **One text line per block element** (`p`, `h1`–`h6`, `li`, `dt`, `dd`, `td`, `th`, `caption`, `figcaption`, `blockquote`, `pre`, `div`, `section`, `aside` and the other HTML block elements); text directly inside a container is a line of its own. **`<br>` and a line feed inside `pre` end the line; the next line's runs get the tag `br`**. White space is folded as a browser shows it (CSS `white-space: normal`): runs of ASCII space, tab, CR, LF and FF become one space, none at a line's start or end; no-break space, U+FEFF, soft hyphen and every other character are kept as stored; `pre` keeps its white space. `head`, `script`, `style` and `template` are not recorded; everything else with text is. Each spine document is one page; ids `p{n}-l{i}` and `p{n}-i{k}` as for PDF. Question: how does markup become lines? Alternative not chosen: keep a `<br>` as "\n" inside one line (a `TextLine` would hold several printed lines, and every later stage would meet "\n"). Reason: lines stay printed lines, as in PDF; the `br` tag tells layout a line continues its element. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 13 Nov 08:10 UTC, under D-80; plan approved as recommended; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-13T08:10:59Z) | sci2audio Issue #73; plan `plans/issue73-epub-plan.md` (project files) §4 (q3); D-63 |
| D-305 | 2026-11-13 | EPUB parser | **The standard library only**: `zipfile` for the container; `xml.etree.ElementTree` for `container.xml`, the package file and the NCX, after refusing any of them that declares an entity; `html.parser` for XHTML content documents and the navigation document (never fails on malformed markup, knows HTML named entities such as `&nbsp;`, never expands custom entities, never fetches). No new dependency. Question: which library reads EPUB? Alternatives not chosen: lxml (BSD-3-Clause; a compiled dependency on three systems whose XML parser resolves entities unless configured); BeautifulSoup4 (MIT; a dependency for what a short handler does); EbookLib (AGPL-3.0, refused by the licence gate); `xml.etree` for XHTML (fails on `&nbsp;` and on any malformed chapter). Reason: robust on real files, safe against entity attacks, no licence row. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 13 Nov 08:10 UTC, under D-80; plan approved as recommended; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-13T08:10:59Z) | sci2audio Issue #73; plan `plans/issue73-epub-plan.md` (project files) §3 (q2) |

Question: how is the work split, given that it touches core, extraction and app? Alternatives not chosen: two PRs without the core fields (loses the anchor, picture path and item order, so #75 would have to guess); one PR across extraction and app (breaks the lane rule; app is single-threaded). | D-295 | 2026-11-13 | How the server finds the audiobook files | The web's audio task writes **`<work>/audiobook.json`** (`"format": "sci2audio-web-audiobook"`, version 1, the document id and the absolute output folder) after a successful job; if it can't, the job still succeeds. The player tries the recorded folder, then the saved output folder, then the server's default. **No path is trusted without checks**: the record must name this document and an absolute folder; the document id must equal `audio.json`'s `source.sha256[:16]` (D-198); file names must be bare names listed in `audio.json`; a file is opened only as `<folder>/<name>` and must resolve to a regular file in that folder (no link out); the read-along file must agree with `audio.json` (duration, chunk count, file names) before any file is served or shown; every span must lie inside its block. New route `GET /api/v1/documents/{id}/audiobook/{name}` serves only those files (Range answers 206). Question: how does the player find the audiobook, which `audio.json` records without its folder? Alternatives not chosen: the output folder in the app's `audio.json` (an app record and its golden file change, and records keep folders out on purpose as machine-specific); always the saved folder (wrong once another document's audiobook went elsewhere, D-246). Reason: a small web-owned record, checked before use, with no change below the web layer. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 13 Nov 08:02 UTC, under D-80; plan approved as recommended; Colin informed in the project chat at 2026-11-13T08:02:38Z) | sci2audio Issue #78, PR [#144](https://github.com/dubidu22/sci2audio/pull/144); plan `plans/issue78-player-plan.md` (project files) §8 (2); D-165, D-192 to D-209 |
