D-47's "everything for offline use" download is the voice plus the spaCy model. The command line (#72) and the server (#76) call one download function in `voices`. Question: how does the voice stay offline after setup when misaki downloads on its own (plan Q12)? Alternative not chosen: a pinned model wheel as a dependency (PyPI rejects direct-URL dependencies). Reason: keeps VA-04 (offline after setup) and the no-network rule true. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 12 Nov 22:39 UTC, under D-80; Colin informed in the project chat at 22:39 UTC); **refined by D-121** (12 Nov: the spaCy wheel is unpacked into the models folder and put on `sys.path`) **and D-122** (12 Nov: `HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1` at all times, because `huggingface_hub` never downloads) | sci2audio Issues #18, #71, #72, #76; plan `plans/v1-remaining-plan.md` (project files) §4; D-47, D-48, D-52, D-54; second opinion `plans/v1-remaining-plan-review.md` |
| D-103 | 2026-11-12 | The conformance suite's place in the layers | Refines the D-46/D-63 layer contract. `sci2audio.testing`, the conformance suite every text step and voice must pass (sci2audio Issue #6, `.claude/rules/tests.md`), imports the stages, so it gets its own place **beside `app`**: the import-linter layer line becomes `sci2audio.app \| sci2audio.testing` (it may import the stages and core; nothing imports it). It **must not import pytest or hypothesis at module import** (plain callables that tests wrap, or an optional extra), so neither becomes a run-time dependency of the package. Question: where does a package that tests the stages sit in the layers (plan Q11)? Alternatives not chosen: under `tests/` only (plugin authors could not run it from the installed package, PE-10 in v1.1); inside a stage (a stage may not import the others). Reason: the suite is shipped code that depends on stages, the same position as `app`. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 12 Nov 22:39 UTC, under D-80; Colin informed in the project chat at 22:39 UTC); **refined by D-114** (12 Nov: `forbidden` contract, cli and web never import `sci2audio.testing`; its path joins lane textprep) | sci2audio Issues #63, #18, #6; plan `plans/v1-remaining-plan.md` (project files) §4; D-46, D-57, D-63, D-69 (a layer-contract change, so critical) |
| D-102 | 2026-11-12 | Read-along on the block text, in a versioned file owned by output | For v1.0, read-along (OU-02) shows **the document's block text** (the original words as layout grouped them) in the browser and highlights the utterance being read; read-along on PDF page images comes after v1.0. The timing data is **one JSON file per job, versioned and owned by the output stage**: for every audio chunk its start and end time, span and chapter. Question: what does read-along show, and who owns its file format (plan Q9)? Alternative not chosen: highlighting on rendered PDF pages (needs page images and box mapping in the interface; more work for the same function). Reason: matches D-61 (6) (script pieces point at original spans) and keeps the format next to the code that writes it. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 12 Nov 22:39 UTC, under D-80; Colin informed in the project chat at 22:39 UTC); **detailed by D-294 to D-303** (13 Nov: the read-along player, #78) | sci2audio Issues #69, #78; plan `plans/v1-remaining-plan.md` (project files) §4 |
| D-101 | 2026-11-12 | Interface technology | The local interface (D-45) is **server-rendered HTML**: FastAPI with Jinja2 templates and a little plain JavaScript, progress by server-sent events through `StreamingResponse`, `python-multipart` for file upload, and **no front-end build step**. The read-along player (sci2audio Issue #78) is the one page with real JavaScript. Licences: FastAPI MIT, uvicorn BSD-3-Clause, Jinja2 BSD-3-Clause, python-multipart Apache-2.0. Question: which technology for the interface (plan Q7)? Alternative not chosen: a JavaScript single-page app with a build step (a second toolchain and more to make accessible). Reason: few dependencies, accessible by default (AQ-01), one language for the whole project. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 12 Nov 22:39 UTC, under D-80; Colin informed in the project chat at 22:39 UTC); **extended by D-195 and D-206** (13 Nov: FastAPI's built-in telemetry switched off; the server's dependency versions) | sci2audio Issues #76, #77, #78; plan `plans/v1-remaining-plan.md` (project files) §4; D-45 |
| D-100 | 2026-11-12 | What a chapter is | An audio chapter (OU-01, UC-03) is **a level-1 section** of the section tree. Text before the first heading is the **first chapter**, titled with the document title from `Content.metadata`, or the file name when the file gives none. EPUB: one chapter per top-level navigation entry. A document without headings is one chapter. **References are deselected by default**; appendices stay selected. **Text steps run on the whole document; the chapter filter applies when the script is cut into utterances; the report marks edits in deselected chapters as not spoken.** Question: what is a chapter, and how do chapter choice and the reference-list step (TP-06) fit together (plan Q6)? Alternatives not chosen: every section at any level (dozens of tiny chapters); filtering chapters before the text steps (the report would lose edits the user may switch back on). Reason: matches how papers are read and keeps the report complete. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 12 Nov 22:39 UTC, under D-80; Colin informed in the project chat at 22:39 UTC); **implemented by D-129 and refined by D-130 to D-133** (12 Nov: chapter boundaries, the first chapter's title, the reference-list names, choosing chapters by index) | sci2audio Issues #70, #69, #72, #77; plan `plans/v1-remaining-plan.md` (project files) §4 |
| D-99 | 2026-11-12 | Where ffmpeg comes from | The output stage gets ffmpeg from **`imageio-ffmpeg`** (BSD-2-Clause wrapper; version 0.8.1 bundles ffmpeg 9.0 binaries, GPL builds, compatible with GPL-3.0-or-later), **with an ffmpeg on PATH as fallback**, so one `uv tool install sci2audio` is enough (WI-01, D-47). imageio-ffmpeg ships **no ffprobe**: files are read back by parsing ffmpeg's own output or with a Python reader. The output PR (sci2audio Issue #69) names the build licence per platform and confirms libmp3lame and aac are compiled in. Question: where does ffmpeg come from (plan Q5)? Alternatives not chosen: requiring a user-installed ffmpeg (breaks the one-command install); PyAV (in-process, larger surface). Reason: one-command install with a known licence, and a fallback for systems where the bundled binary does not run. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 12 Nov 22:39 UTC, under D-80; Colin informed in the project chat at 22:39 UTC) | sci2audio Issues #69, #80; plan `plans/v1-remaining-plan.md` (project files) §4; D-47; second opinion `plans/v1-remaining-plan-review.md` |
| D-98 | 2026-11-12 | Who cuts the listening script into utterances | The **textprep stage owns the utterance cutter**: it cuts the listening script (`ScriptPiece`s, D-61 (6)) into sentence-sized spans inside one block, one `AudioChunk` per utterance; the voice never sees a `Document`. Line breaks inside a block become spaces in the cutter, not edits; a hyphen at a line end is an edit (TP-02). Kokoro has an input-length limit per call, so **a sentence that is too long gets a fallback cut at clause marks** (commas, semicolons, colons). No core change. Question: who splits the script into sentences for the voice and read-along, and are line breaks edits (plan Q4)? Alternatives not chosen: the voice splits (it would need document structure, and read-along spans would depend on the engine); one edit per line break (thousands of edits that change nothing a listener hears). Reason: the cutter needs the same language knowledge as the text steps and must give read-along stable spans. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 12 Nov 22:39 UTC, under D-80; Colin informed in the project chat at 22:39 UTC); **refined by D-115** (12 Nov: textprep's own `Utterance`, `max_chars` 300 by default, fallback cut order `;`/`:`, comma, dash, space) | sci2audio Issues #63, #71; plan `plans/v1-remaining-plan.md` (project files) §4; D-57, D-61 |
| D-97 | 2026-11-12 | Where footnotes are read | Refines feature TP-05 ("footnote marks and footnote text"), which named no place. **Footnote text is read where layout placed it** (the column foot, in reading order), **introduced by its mark** ("Footnote 1."); **footnote marks are removed from the running text**; **one category switch leaves all footnotes out**. Moving a note next to its mark would need an edit across blocks, which D-61 (1) rules out; that waits for after v1.0. Question: where does the listener hear a footnote (plan Q2)? Alternatives not chosen: next to its mark (needs a core change); always left out (loses content the author wrote). Reason: possible with the current core, faithful, and switchable. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 12 Nov 22:39 UTC, under D-80; Colin informed in the project chat at 22:39 UTC) | sci2audio Issue #64; plan `plans/v1-remaining-plan.md` (project files) §4; D-61, D-81 (footnote blocks), feature list TP-05 |
| D-96 | 2026-11-12 | Plan for the remaining v1.0 work; batched plan approval; #47 into v1.0 | The coordinator approves the plan for everything left before v1.0 (`plans/v1-remaining-plan.md`, project files) **with changes** from a second-opinion review (`plans/v1-remaining-plan-review.md`). The plan maps all 41 Must features to issues (sci2audio Issues #63 to #80 filed for the uncovered work), orders them in five waves, and names the critical path #63 → #70 → #71 → #76 → #77 → #78/#79 → #80. Process: **D-69 stays as it is**, although nearly every remaining PR is critical because it adds golden files or English regression cases; to avoid waiting, **the plans of the text-rule issues #64 to #68 are approved together, in one batch, once #63 merges**; each PR still gets its own review thread (D-68). **Issue #47** (a flaky property test the nightly run can hit) **moves into the v1.0 milestone** and is fixed first. Under D-80, the review thread of #39 checks every new truth sentence against the PDF instead of Colin. Question: how is the remaining work ordered, and how do approvals keep up (plan Q1, Q14)? Alternative not chosen: relaxing D-69 for regression cases (they are what protects the listener from silent changes). Reason: keeps the strict process while removing five approval waits. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 12 Nov 22:39 UTC, under D-80; Colin informed in the project chat at 22:39 UTC) | sci2audio Issues #4 to #12, #18, #24, #33, #36, #39, #47, #48, #58, #63 to #80; D-68, D-69, D-80, D-95 |
| D-95 | 2026-11-12 | Layout Issue #21 ends with step 3; milestones for the deferred layout issues | **Issue #21 (layout) has no step 4**: it is complete once sci2audio PR #61 (layout step 3 PR D2) merges, and its leftovers are tracked as separate issues. **Milestones** for the layout issues deferred from the step 3 reviews: #33 (body-size running heads), #48 (ACL checklist titles not found as headings) and #58 (a loose rule or mark joins the block read before it, even inside another block) **move into v1.0**, alongside #36, because each changes what a listener hears; #32, #49, #54, #55, #56, #59 and #60 **stay without a milestone**, since they are rare in the corpus or only about speed; #25 and #41 **remain v1.1**. **Next after layout**: wiring the stages together, since nothing calls the layout stage yet. Question: does #21 need a step 4, and which deferred layout issues must be fixed before v1.0? Alternatives not chosen: a step 4 inside #21 for the leftovers (one issue that never closes, mixing unrelated fixes); putting every deferred layout issue in v1.0 (holds the release for rare cases and speed work). Reason: steps 1 to 3 meet the plan of D-67 as widened by D-81; a milestone goes to the issues that change the spoken output. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 12 Nov 21:50 UTC, under D-80; Colin informed in the project chat at 21:50 UTC) | sci2audio Issue #21, PR #61; Issues #25, #32, #33, #36, #41, #48, #49, #54, #55, #56, #58, #59, #60; D-67, D-80, D-81 |
| D-94 | 2026-11-12 | Two deviations from the D-93 plan in the formulas PR (layout PR D2) | Accepts two changes to the approved D-93 plan, made in sci2audio PR #61 (layout step 3 PR D2). (1) **`LayoutSettings.math_share` defaults to 0.4, not 0.5**: nowak's restated Eq. (9) has 0.45 of its letters in a math font, so 0.5 would miss it as an unnumbered formula. (2) **A wide formula ends the paragraph above it**: a formula set across both columns (a band element, #34) cuts the open paragraph like any other formula; only a formula at the top of a later page, after a paragraph that runs on from an earlier page, lets the paragraph run on past it (the nowak p4 to p5 sentence still joins up). Found while explaining the golden diff: under D-93 point 4 a sentence on nowak p9 was read before formula (21). Covered by the new test `test_a_formula_across_both_columns_mid_page_ends_the_paragraph_before_it`. Question: may PR #61 depart from D-93 on these two points? Alternatives not chosen: keeping 0.5 (misses a truth formula); letting every wide formula carry the paragraph on, as D-93 point 4 said (reads text out of printed order). Reason: both changes meet the D-93 truth targets (45 of 45 numbered and 6 of 6 unnumbered formulas, 28 of 28 verbatim sentences) and keep the formula where it is printed. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 12 Nov 21:48 UTC, under D-80; Colin informed in the project chat at 21:48 UTC) | sci2audio PR #61, Issues #21, #34; D-80, D-93 |
| D-93 | 2026-11-12 | Display formulas as FORMULA placeholders (layout PR D2) and the fix for #34 | Approves the plan for layout step 3 PR D2 (`plans/issue21-step3-prD2-formulas.md`, project files) with all five recommended choices. (1) **A formula stands in place**: the paragraph holding it is cut into the text before it, the FORMULA, and the text after it as a new PARAGRAPH, each line's text unchanged (consistent with D-92 point 4, a placeholder stands where its first line was read). (2) **arsiwalla Eq. (4) on p16**, a picture with an equation number, is a FORMULA labelled "4" that holds the picture; the corpus figure test gets a one-line exception (`NUMBERED_AS_FORMULAS`), FIGURE blocks go from 18 to 17, and the truth file is unchanged. (3) **Aligned equations with several numbers** (nowak 16a–16f, 10 and 11) give one FORMULA per printed number, each row going to the nearest number row by vertical centre, so `Block.label` stays a single value. (4) **#34 is fixed in the reading-order step**, anchored on an equation number at the text area's right edge: a number-anchored group wider than `wide` becomes one element that cuts the page into bands, and it is held as a FORMULA so the open paragraph runs on across it (the nowak p4 to p5 sentence joins up). (5) **Unnumbered formulas are detected conservatively**: display groups in PARAGRAPH blocks only, after captions have taken their floats, with at least `math_share` of their letters in a math font or of Unicode category Sm; display math in footnotes and list items is left as it is. Labels are stored as printed, without brackets ("16a", "A.2"). New settings, all in `LayoutSettings`: `formula_indent` = 2 em, `number_reach` = 0.5 em, `math_share` = 0.5, `math_fonts` (a tuple of font names); `float_gap`, `wide`, `fill_slack`, `size_change` and `row_overlap` are reused. Truth targets: 45 numbered formulas with their labels in printed order per page, 6 unnumbered formulas, no FORMULA in chi, hardt, seaborn or pymedphys, verbatim sentences 28 of 28 (`NOT_YET_ONE_BLOCK` empty); all D1 counts unchanged except FIGURE 17. No core or layer-contract change: critical only because the golden file changes (D-69). Conditions of the approval: every golden change explained by cause in the PR body; any truth verbatim sentence cut by a formula named in the PR with an Issue; the nightly Hypothesis profile run before the PR opens; any tuning of defaults away from the plan stated in the PR body. Question: how do display formulas become FORMULA placeholder blocks, and how is #34 (a numbered equation spanning both columns read as pieces of each) fixed? Alternatives not chosen: (1) holding the formula until its paragraph ends, as figures are (moves the formula after text that follows it on the page); (2) keeping the FIGURE and making "(4)" an empty FORMULA, or giving FIGURE a label (the picture loses its number, or a core change); (3) one block for a whole aligned display (needs several labels, a core change); (4) a general rule merging narrow pieces that cross the gutter (risks merging table cells or figure labels); (5) a wider net for unnumbered formulas. Reason: placing the formula where it is printed is faithful to the page and keeps it next to where it is referenced; one block per printed number lets "Eq. (16c)" point at one block; anchoring #34 on the number keeps the order change specific; a missed unnumbered formula stays a paragraph with its text unchanged, while a false one turns text into a placeholder that is not read aloud (D-55), the worse failure. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 12 Nov 21:21 UTC, under D-80; Colin informed in the project chat at 21:21 UTC); **partly revised by D-94** (12 Nov): `math_share` is 0.4, and a wide formula ends the paragraph above it unless it stands at the top of a later page | sci2audio Issues #21, #34; plan `plans/issue21-step3-prD2-formulas.md` (project files); D-55, D-69, D-81, D-88, D-92 |
| D-92 | 2026-11-12 | Refinements to layout PR D1 graphics | Revises D-88 to D-91 for layout PR D1, after coding showed five cases the approved plan did not settle. (1) **A figure no caption names takes no text**: without a caption, a frame or page background around running text cannot be told from a figure, so its text stays paragraphs (open risk tracked in sci2audio Issue #55); a captioned figure takes its text through the caption's walk. (2) **Arrows, frame sides and icons are handled like a lone rule**: an uncaptioned drawing at most `line_width` thick (a rule, an arrow, a frame side) or a graphic at most `mark_height` tall (an icon, a small logo) joins the block read just before it, text unchanged, as D-90 rules for a lone rule; the truth says logos and icons are not figures. (3) **A caption reads toward the side whose walk takes a graphic**, otherwise toward the nearer side; algorithm captions read downward. Nearest-only gets nowak Figure 2 wrong (the paragraph below its caption is 8 pt away, its tree above 13 pt). (4) **A placeholder stands where its first text line was read**, so an algorithm follows its caption rather than its box's top rule. (5) **White inside a ruled table**: white of up to `caption_reach` is crossed when a rule as wide as one already taken lies ahead, with only takeable items up to it (chi Table 4 has 13 pt of white inside each row). Settings, all in `LayoutSettings`: `caption_reach` = 2 em, `float_gap` = 1 em (the row gap and figure text reach), `line_width` = 1 em (in place of the planned `rule_height`, since arrows and frame sides count too), `mark_height` = 2 em; `repeat_pages`, `head_drift`, `size_change` and `row_overlap` are reused. Question: how to settle the cases the D1 plan left open without changing any text? Alternatives not chosen: uncaptioned figures absorbing the text inside their box (swallowed running text inside frames); nearest-side captions only (fails nowak Figure 2); icons and arrows as FIGURE blocks (contradicts the truth). Reason: each choice meets the truth counts (10 TABLE, 18 FIGURE, 29 of 29 captions describing a block of their kind) while footnotes, headings, captions and list items stay unchanged, and every block keeps its lines as found. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 12 Nov 20:04 UTC, under D-80; Colin informed in the project chat at 20:04 UTC) | sci2audio Issue #21; plan `plans/issue21-step3-prD-placeholders.md` (project files); D-88, D-89, D-90, D-91; sci2audio Issues #55, #56 |
| D-91 | 2026-11-12 | A graphic repeated on every page is running-head furniture | In layout PR D1, a picture or drawing group with the same box on at least `repeat_pages` pages (the JOSS logo on every page) becomes a FURNITURE block with role RUNNING_HEAD, as a repeated line does. Question: what is a logo or icon repeated on every page, given that the truth says logos are not figures and every item must be in a block? Alternative not chosen: a new furniture role for graphics, which changes the core dataclasses (critical) for something the listener never hears either way. Reason: a repeated graphic at a page edge is a running head in every sense the stage uses; the existing role keeps core unchanged. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 12 Nov 19:25 UTC, under D-80; Colin informed in the project chat at 19:25 UTC) | sci2audio Issue #21; plan `plans/issue21-step3-prD-placeholders.md` (project files); D-79, D-81 |
| D-90 | 2026-11-12 | A lone rule joins the block read before it | In layout PR D1, a thin rule (at most `rule_height` thick) that belongs to no table, algorithm or footnote (a page-foot rule, a separator) is not a FIGURE: it joins the block read just before it in reading order, as a part whose start is the end of that block's text, so the text is unchanged. A footnote rule joins the first footnote below it (sci2audio Issue #51); a rule inside a table or algorithm joins that block. Question: what happens to the about 125 thin lines that today each become an empty FIGURE block? Alternatives not chosen: a FIGURE block of their own (the truth counts 15 figures, and each would become a spoken placeholder), or a new furniture role (a core change). Reason: every item must sit in exactly one block, a rule carries no text, and joining it to its neighbour keeps it findable without being read. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 12 Nov 19:25 UTC, under D-80; Colin informed in the project chat at 19:25 UTC); **partly revised by D-92** (12 Nov): `line_width` replaces `rule_height`, and uncaptioned arrows, frame sides, icons and small logos also join the block before them; **refined by D-329 to D-333** (13 Nov): a group whose box lies inside one text line's box stands in that line's block, just after the line | sci2audio Issue #21; plan `plans/issue21-step3-prD-placeholders.md` (project files); Issue #51; D-81; D-92 |
| D-89 | 2026-11-12 | nowak Figure 1 is one figure block that three captions describe | In layout PR D1, a figure that holds several algorithms (nowak Figure 1: Algorithms 1 and 2 side by side) is **one FIGURE block** holding both listings, the left one first, each read line by line. Alternative not chosen: one FIGURE block per algorithm, with Figure 1's caption describing only the first, so the figure's second half is not named. Reason: one placeholder can announce "Figure 1, with Algorithms 1 and 2", and a reference to any of the three labels finds the same block. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 12 Nov 19:25 UTC, under D-80; Colin informed in the project chat at 19:25 UTC) | sci2audio Issue #21; plan `plans/issue21-step3-prD-placeholders.md` (project files); D-81 (algorithms are figures), D-86 |

(4) becomes a FORMULA | sci2audio Issue #21; plan `plans/issue21-step3-prD-placeholders.md` (project files); D-81, D-86; Issues #34, #38, #51; D2 plan: D-93 |
| D-87 | 2026-11-12 | Three refinements to captions and list items (layout PR C2) | Revises D-81 and D-85 for PR C2 of layout step 3. **(1)** An algorithm's caption ends where its numbered pseudocode lines ("1: …") start; those lines stay a PARAGRAPH until PR D handles pseudocode (sci2audio Issue #38). **(2) Set alike:** the items of one list all have their markers standing apart from their text (a piece of its own, with a gap) or none do. **(3)** A list marker needs text after it on the same line, so a display formula's number on its own line is not a list item (formula numbers belong to PR D). Question: what keeps captions and lists from swallowing pseudocode, numbered linguistic examples and formula numbers? Alternatives not chosen: captions running to the end of their block (nowak's Algorithm 3 caption held its first pseudocode line); a rule on the first word after a marker (a lowercase word such as "is"), which would also reject real list items that start in lower case; and leaving formula numbers to be read as markers. Reason: hardt p1 sets numbered examples apart ("(1)", gap, "William went running.") around a paragraph that opens by naming one ("(1) is understood as …"); without (2), that paragraph and the next example made a list. Nowak p2's display-formula numbers come out as "(1)\nX −→ a", and without (3) they made a list. With all three: 29 of 29 truth caption labels, 69 list items, headings unchanged at 73 of 75. Captions and lists run before headings because they can cut blocks; headings look only at paragraphs. | **Decided (Claude)** (coordinator, 12 Nov 19:21 UTC, under D-80; Colin informed in the project chat at 19:21 UTC) | sci2audio Issue #21, PR #53 (C2); plan `plans/issue21-step3-prC2-captions-lists.md` (project files); D-81, D-85, D-86; Issue #38 |

| D-82 | 2026-11-12 | Bold and italic from CM-Super and EC font names | PDF extraction reads bold and italic from the cut in CM-Super and EC font names (the T1 Computer Modern most LaTeX papers use), at the start of the name or after a subset prefix, followed by the four-digit design size. Bold cuts: bx, bl, bi, xc, oc, rb, bm, sx, so. Italic or slanted cuts: sl, ti, ci, sc, bl, bi, oc, si, so, it, st, vi. Upright italic (ui) and the other cuts stay plain. The lists come from the cm-super AFM files on CTAN: a cut is bold if its file says Weight Bold, and italic if its ItalicAngle is not 0. Question: PDFium reports a normal weight and no bold flag for these fonts (e.g. SFBX1095), so their runs came out not bold; should extraction read the style from the name? Alternative not chosen: leave such runs unstyled, which loses the headings of papers set in these fonts (arsiwalla's "Abstract"). Reason: extraction already reads Computer Modern names (CMBX, CMTI); the pattern is narrow (it needs `sf` or `ec`, a known cut and four digits, so SF Pro is untouched). In the corpus it changes only 38 runs, which become bold. | **Decided (Claude)** (layout thread, plan accepted by the coordinator 12 Nov 18:03 UTC, under D-80; Colin informed in the project chat) | sci2audio Issue #45, PR #46; D-63 |

| D-61 | 2026-11-12 | Core data model (Issue #17) | Choices made while writing `sci2audio.core` (sci2audio PR #19): (1) a `Span` lies inside one block (block index, character offsets); an edit never crosses blocks, so removing a whole reference list is one edit per block; (2) edits may not overlap at all, not even two at the same position; core has no priority rules, the app chooses one before applying (`OverlappingEditsError` names both steps); (3) an `ask` edit reads the original until the user decides; the run never stops to ask; an answered ask becomes `replace` or `remove`; (4) an empty span marks a position: `pause` needs one, `replace` on one inserts text; (5) table and formula notices (D-55) come from a text step as a `replace`, not from core; placeholder blocks keep whatever text extraction found; (6) `apply_edits` returns `ScriptPiece`s (spoken text plus the original span), not a plain string, so read-along (OU-02) and chapters map audio back to the source; (7) a block lists the extracted items it is made of (item id plus the character offset where its text starts), replacing the first plan's page regions (D-63); captions link to the block they describe; (8) the golden-file export is JSON-compatible plain data with sorted keys and defaults left out, not YAML, so core stays standard library only; the source keeps the file name and SHA-256, never a path. Left for later issues: footnote-mark links (TP-05), section types (TP-16), pause length, per-character PDF positions | **Decided** (Colin, 12 Nov: merged sci2audio PRs #20 and #19 and chose "Yes, mark D-61 Decided" on the decision card); **carried out by D-558** (14 Nov): a footnote is read after the sentence holding its mark | sci2audio PR #19, Issue #17 |
